Best approach: parallelize over z (and possibly y) with collapse. Given nx,ny,nz variable and default 128^3, use `#pragma omp parallel for collapse(2)` over z and y perhaps. But cache behavior: x innermost is contiguous. Better parallelize z with collapse(2) (z,y). OpenMP should be able to vectorize inner x loop. For maximum scalability, collapse(2) over z,y gives enough parallelism.\n\n- Boundary copy loop is O(N) and has conditions. It runs every iteration. Can parallelize it too. But boundary copy has significant overhead relative to interior. We can optimize semantics equivalently. But requirement \"maintaining correctness and equivalent semantics.\" We can rewrite boundary copy in a more efficient way but the result must be identical. Simplest: parallelize interior with collapse(2) and boundary copy with parallel for over flat index. That's fine.\n\nActually, a bigger performance concern: The original boundary copy loops over ALL points checking boundary conditions, writing boundary. This is O(N) with branch. Could optimize by copying only boundary faces. The result identical. Let me implement efficiently:\n\nBoundary consists of:\n- z==0 and z==nz-1 planes: full x,y\n- y==0 and y==ny-1 for interior z: full x\n- x==0 and x==nx-1 for interior y,z\n\nBut wait: original condition uses `x == 0 || x == nx-1 || y == 0 || y == ny-1 || z == 0 || z == nz-1`.
